[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
17. Changes in project grading


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
(wef now) and Eligibility to enter the BSc Level (wef May 2024)


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
Here are two changes being effected with respect to project grading and eligibility to enter the degree level.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. Project grading:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
So far - students had to complete all feature requirements given for a project, pass the L1 viva and move onto the L2 viva and pass that too to get a pass grade.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
Looking into the data we have, we understand that all students may not be equally proficient in the areas of programming, machine learning and business and excel in all 4 projects.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
Hence the grading is being changed as follows:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
MAD1, MAD2, MLP projects: WEF current term


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. For MAD1 and MAD2 projects, when the submitted Application passes the check for Appdev, L1 viva will be scheduled.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
For MLP project, if the cutoff is crossed in the Kaggle contest and the student declares that all the models have been completed, the L1 viva is scheduled.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. There are 2 cutoff marks that will be decided for the L1 level for each term:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. Cutoff1: X, Cutoff2: Y;
2. X<Y, X>0


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. The L1 viva will be conducted and scoring will be done based on the rubrics defined for it. Based on the marks scored in L1 viva (L1_S), the following are the possibilities:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. If (L1_S < X): Student has to resubmit the project. A few days are given for them to resubmit in the same term. If the second time also, L1_S<X, they will have to redo the project in the next term.
2. If (X <= L1_S < Y), then students are asked some additional questions for assessing their knowledge and awarded D or E grade. Such students do not go in for L2 viva at all. They will have a pass grade.
3. If (L1_S >= Y), L2 viva is scheduled for them.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
1. If they pass the viva cutoff for L2, they get a grade including L1 and L2 scores.
2. If they do not pass the L2 viva cutoff, they get one more chance at L2 viva.
3. If they do not pass in the second attempt at L2 viva, they get an appropriate grade ( D or E) based on their L1 performance alone.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
BDM project evaluation


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
So far: The process has been identifying the business, collecting data, analysing and sharing their insights.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
Going forward from Jan 2024:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
Students will be allowed to web scrape data and analyse it, sharing the source from where the data was taken. The BDM team will outline the steps involved.The maximum grade that the student can earn in this case will be D.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
For those students who completed diplomas with less than 4 standalone projects (in Sep 2021/Jan 2022 terms projects were included in theory courses), whatever projects were done standalone will be counted for the average CGPA of 7.0. For example, if 2 projects were done as part of the theory and 2 projects standalone, then only the grades in the two standalone projects will be used to compute the project CGPA. The projects done as part of the theory courses will not be counted.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
For those who do 4 standalone projects, eligibility to enter degree level is illustrated below with some examples:


[TYPE: table]
[SECTION: 17. Changes in project grading]
[HEADERS: P1, P2, P3, P4, Total points, Eligible to enter the degree level?]
[ROW: E, E, S, S, 28, Yes]
[ROW: E, E, S, A, 27, No]
[ROW: D, E, S, S/A/B, 30/29/28, Yes]
[ROW: D, D, S/A/B, S/A/B, 28-32, Yes]
[ROW: D, D, C, C, 26, No]


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18. New Rules for Foundation & Diploma Level Completion]
18. New Rules for Foundation & Diploma Level Completion


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.1 Foundation level checkpoints:]
18.1 Foundation level checkpoints:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.1 Foundation level checkpoints:]
These conditions shall be applicable to the students admitted to the foundation level from May 2024. These are not applicable for students who have entered in Jan 2024 term or before that. But completing the foundation level in 9 terms is applicable for all. Terms classified under RKA and terms where all subjects have been dropped will also be included in the total term count.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.1 Foundation level checkpoints:]
Students will be removed from the program if they do not complete


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.1 Foundation level checkpoints:]
1. at least 4 courses within 4 terms.
2. at least 6 courses within 6 terms.
3. the 8 foundation courses within 9 terms.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.1 Foundation level checkpoints:]
The student will be issued warnings about insufficient progress and about complying with these conditions starting from term 3.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.2 Change in CCC at the foundation level:]
18.2 Change in CCC at the foundation level:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.2 Change in CCC at the foundation level:]
This is for ALL students in the foundation level.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.2 Change in CCC at the foundation level:]
Currently, the CCC is calculated based on the courses passed in the last 2 terms by the students and enforced too, deciding the number of courses that students can take in a given term in the Foundation Level.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.2 Change in CCC at the foundation level:]
In view of the conditions imposed above leading to exit from the program if not satisfied, it was proposed that the CCC will only be a recommendation and not enforced. This will enable students to make up for term breaks and meet the proposed course completion criteria.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.3 Increasing the maximum number of terms for completing the diploma level]
18.3 Increasing the maximum number of terms for completing the diploma level


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.3 Increasing the maximum number of terms for completing the diploma level]
The Diploma level can now be completed in a maximum of 12 terms instead of 9 terms as has been the case till now. This extension of 12 terms as the maximum limit to complete the Diploma level shall be applied for all learners, starting from the first batch, who started the Diploma Level in September 2021. Terms classified under RKA and terms where all subjects have been dropped will also be included in the total term count.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.3 Increasing the maximum number of terms for completing the diploma level]
The maximum duration for the BSc and BS levels shall remain unchanged at 9 terms. The total duration permissible for the BSc and BS degrees shall also remain at 6 and 8 years, respectively.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level]
18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level]
These conditions below shall be applicable to the students admitted to the Diploma level from May 2024. These are not applicable for students who have entered in Jan 2024 term or before that.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level]
To ensure continuous progress and steer students towards completing courses on time, the following intermediate checkpoints were also proposed. The student must complete


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level]
1. at least 3 courses and 1 project in the first 3 terms from starting the Diploma level - if not, they will be put on academic probation and given a warning.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level]
—This warning is intended for students to structure their study plans suitably. Students can still register for courses in the subsequent terms; but they must be mindful that they have to complete 6 courses and 2 projects within 6 terms from the time they started the Diploma level.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.4 Checkpoints in the Diploma level]
1. at least 6 courses and 2 projects in the first 6 terms from starting the Diploma level - if not, they will be removed from the program.
2. at least 9 courses and 3 projects in the first 9 terms from starting the Diploma level - if not, they will be removed from the program.
3. All 12 courses and 4 projects in 12 terms from starting the Diploma level - if not, they will be removed from the program The student will be issued sufficient alerts and warnings about these checkpoints.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Updated on May 17, 2026


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Scenario 1: Students who will not be able to Meet the Academic Milestone Requirements


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Students who will not be able to satisfy the required academic milestone even after registering for the maximum number of permitted theory/projects will not be allowed for registration in that term.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
For such students:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
1. A notification message will be displayed on the first page of the course registration portal indicating that course registration is not permitted for the current term due to inability to meet the academic milestone requirements.
2. Notifications will also be communicated through System alerts, eMail and WhatsApp.
3. The student email ID will be deactivated after providing a notice period of one month.
4. The student ID card will also be revoked in the same term. Note : Students who are in the FTQ term and could not meet the CGPA requirements tp enter to the degree level as mentioned in Section 19 will have to exit in the FTQ term itself. They cannot take improvement of theory and project once the milestone terms are completed or reached.


[TYPE: table]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
[HEADERS: Timeline of Action, When will it be done?, What will be done?]
[ROW: Course Registration Restriction Notification, After the End-Term results are released and two days before course registration, Students will be notified that course registration is not permitted for the current term.]
[ROW: FTQ Letter, After the End-Term results are released and two days before course registration, The program removal letter will be issued and made live in the student dashboard.]
[ROW: Student mail id deactivation, One month from Registration restriction notification, Student mail id will be deactivated. Students must take backup of any data they have kept saved in the student mail id drive]


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Scenario 2: Students in the Final Milestone Term Not Registering for the required Theory/Projects for the Milestone


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Students who are in their final term to complete the milestone requirements must ensure that they register for the required number of theory/projects needed to complete the milestone in that term.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
If the students does not register for the required number of theory / projects :


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
1. After the course registration window closes, the ops team will review registrations and identify students who have not registered for the required number of theory/projects. Such students will be informed within 3 working days after the closure of course registration.
2. Students must respond within 3 days from the date of communication.
3. If a student does not respond within the stipulated timeline:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
1. The registration will be cancelled.
2. Refund will be processed as per applicable norms.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
1. If the student responds within the timeline:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
1. They will be permitted to register for the remaining required theory/projects.
2. The student may then continue in the program subject to satisfying the milestone requirements.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
5. If the student drops courses in the drop window it will not be considered.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Students are strongly advised to verify their completed theory/projects and ensure appropriate registration during every term. Note : Students who are in the FTQ term and could not meet the CGPA requirements tp enter to the degree level as mentioned in Section 19 will have to exit in the FTQ term itself. They cannot take improvement of theory and project once the milestone terms are completed or reached.


[TYPE: table]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
[HEADERS: Timeline of Action, When will it be done?, What will be done?]
[ROW: Course Registration announcement, When the course registration begins, Notification on the required number of theory / projects is sent over mail, system alerts.]
[ROW: Registration review  (A), Within three days from registration closes, The students who have not registered for the required number of theory/projects will be shortlisted. Pending payment corresponding to the mandatory courses to register will be enabled and notified.]
[ROW: Registration review  (B), Two days after the pending payment deadline is passed, The registration for the students who have not paid will be revoked, refund is initiated and moved to the mandatory exit category.]
[ROW: FTQ letter, After the student status is marked as mandatory exit i.e two days from Registration review  (B), The program removal letter will be issued and made live in the student dashboard.]
[ROW: Student mail id deactivation, One month from Registration review  (B), Student mail id will be deactivated. Students must take backup of any data they have kept saved in the student mail id drive.]


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Scenario 3: Students in the term preceding to Final Milestone Term Not Registering for the required Projects for the Milestone [updated on July 02,2026] To ensure timely completion of milestone requirements, the required Project courses (2, 3, or 4 projects, as applicable) corresponding to the 6th, 9th, or 12th milestone term must be registered in the term immediately preceding the respective milestone term.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Accordingly, students are strongly advised to plan their course registrations such that  the required Theory and Projects are completed in the term preceding the applicable milestone term.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Planning course registrations in this manner provides students with a one-term buffer to complete the milestone without last minute academic pressure.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
If the students does not register for the required number of  projects,the process will be  :


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
1. After the course registration window closes, the ops team will review registrations and identify students who have not registered for the required number of projects.
2. The required number of pending Projects will be updated in the Looker Studio dashboard.
3. The payment for the applicable Projects (updated in the Looker studio) will be enabled on the student's dashboard. Students must complete the payment within the specified deadline.
4. Once the payment deadline has passed, the Operations Team will consolidate the list of students who have successfully completed the payment. Subsequently, the corresponding Project course cards and access to the Project portal will be enabled for these students.
5. Students who have not completed the payment will have to plan to complete the pending projects in the milestone term to avoid removal from the program.
6. If the student drops courses in the drop window it will not be considered.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
The preceding term of final term here indicates the 5th/8th/11th term of diploma level for 6th term / 9th term and 12th term milestone.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Recommendation for Milestone Completion [Updated on Sep 7,2026] 1. Complete Milestone requirement in preceding term :


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Students are recommended to complete the courses required to fulfil their academic-level milestone requirements in the term preceding their milestone term. This provides an opportunity to register for and complete any pending or failed courses in the milestone term, ensuring that the milestone requirements are met on time. Eg : If the 6th milestone term is Jan 2027 then have the milestone requirements completed by the end of 5th term. 2 . Do not take Projects in the milestone term :


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.5 Checkpoints in the Foundation and Diploma level]
Students are recommended to complete the projects required to fulfil their academic-level milestone requirements in the term preceding their milestone term. This provides an opportunity to register for and complete any pending or failed projects in the milestone term, ensuring that the milestone requirements are met on time.It is highly not recommended to take projects in the last term. Eg: If Jan 2027 is your 6th term from Diploma start, try to register and complete at least 2 projects by Sep 2026. 3. Students who wish to complete the Diploma in Data Science by opting for the BDM Project are recommended to register for the BDM Project by their 2nd term at the Diploma level. Based on the observed trend, students typically take at least 2–3 terms to complete the BDM Project, considering the multiple stages involved and the possibility of re-submissions. There have also been instances where students were unable to meet the Diploma-level milestone requirements because they registered for the BDM Project only in their milestone term and could not complete the project within the available timeframe. Therefore, early registration is strongly recommended to allow sufficient time for completion of all stages and any required re-submissions.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
To enter into degree level w.e.f May 2024 onwards i.e This rule shall be applicable to all students entering the degree level (BSc) from the May 2024 term.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
1. For students who have entered the program before May 2026 term


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
For a student to progress to the degree level (BSc) of the Data Science and Applications program:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
i) CGPA at the end of the diploma level >= 6.0 AND ii) CGPA of the projects in diploma level >= 7.0 For those students who completed diplomas with less than 4 standalone projects (in Sep 2021/Jan 2022 terms projects were included in theory courses), whatever projects were done standalone will be counted for the average CGPA of 7.0. For example, if 2 projects were done as part of the theory and 2 projects standalone, then only the grades in the two standalone projects will be used to compute the project CGPA. The projects done as part of the theory courses will not be counted.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
1. For students who have entered the program from May 2026 term onwards


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
[updated June 4, 2026]


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
- Students should complete the foundation+diploma levels in a total of 5 years (15 terms) AND
- CGPA at the end of the diploma level >= 6.0 AND
- CGPA of the projects in diploma level >= 7.0


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
- Those who do not complete the foundation+diploma levels in a total of 5 years (15 terms) from the starting of the foundation level can utilise the additional available 2 years to complete the diploma level but cannot proceed to the degree level . They have to exit at the diploma level.
- Those who do not satisfy the CGPA criteria can repeat courses and projects for grade improvement if they are within the limit of 15 terms from program start. If they are in the 15th term and do not match the required CGPA, they still have to exit with the diploma.
- All skipped terms and RKA also will be included in the milestone calculation.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
1. BSc completion timeline : The below information is approved by the steering committee and yet to be approved by the senate. (Updated on Sep 25, 2026) An important change in the BSc completion timeline for the BS in Data Science and Applications programme.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
The existing requirement to complete the BSc degree completion within 6 years from the date of joining the program has been removed.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
Students will now have an overall period of 8 years to complete the applicable qualification (BSc/BS/PGD/MTech).


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
Please note that the existing milestones and eligibility requirements applicable to the Foundation and Diploma levels will continue to remain in effect as per the respective batch policies.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.6 Checkpoints in entering the Degree level]
Students are advised to take note of this change and plan their academic progression accordingly.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 18.7 Improvement Term Limit]
18.7 Improvement Term Limit


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.7 Improvement Term Limit]
[Updated on Aug 28,2025]


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.7 Improvement Term Limit]
As per the new policy from Jan 2026 Term, the students can improve the grades obtained in courses of a level as long as they are within the same level and have not progressed to the next level. For eg: if a student completes the 8 courses of the foundation level and wishes to improve any course grade, they have to do it in the terms that are available within the maximum permissible terms. The student will not be allowed to take any foundation level improvement course once they start the Diploma Level. The cgpa will be frozen once they move to a new level.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18.7 Improvement Term Limit]
We don't recommend taking improvement courses in the Sep 2025 term itself.

or when graduating with BS.

[TYPE: paragraph]

when graduating with BS will be identified and awarded. The actual number of students

[TYPE: paragraph]

[TYPE: paragraph]

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]
prizes.


[TYPE: paragraph]

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]
considered as a separate category and will be eligible for prizes with medals being given for the students for the respective category.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]
Prize Eligibility Criteria for Improvement Courses


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]
For the prizes awarded after the diploma level, we will consider the CGPA only till the term when the student completes the two diplomas. Improvements in foundational/diploma courses done after completion of both diplomas will not be considered for the diploma-level prizes. For example, if a student had repeated a foundation/diploma course for improvement in September 2023 but had completed both diplomas in May 2023, then the CGPA secured in May 2023 will be considered for diploma-level prize. Diploma-level prizes will not be given retrospectively.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]
Certificate of Merit and Distinction Policy


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 19. Eligibility Criteria Prize]
Students eligible for a Certificate of Merit or Distinction must maintain a clean academic and disciplinary record. Any instance of academic malpractice or disciplinary violation in a student's history will result in disqualification from receiving the certificate, irrespective of academic performance. [Updated on April 14,2025]

20.1 Eligibility and Requirements for Re-admission Final Exams

To rejoin the IITM BS program and continue their studies, students who previously exited with an original certificate must take the final exams for specific subjects detailed in the table below. These subjects are determined by the certificate they hold and the level at which they wish to re-enroll.


[TYPE: table]
[SECTION: 20.1 Eligibility and Requirements for Re-admission Final Exams]
[HEADERS: Original Certificate taken and exited, Level they wish to join, Subjects for Mandatory Final Exams]
[ROW: Diploma in Programming, Diploma in Data Science, MAD2, PDSA]
[ROW: Diploma in Data Science, Diploma in programming, MLT, MLP]
[ROW: Both Diplomas, BSc level, MAD2, PDSA, MLT, MLP]
[ROW: BSc, BS level, Deep Learning (DL), Artificial Intelligence (AI), Software Engineering, Software Testing]
[ROW: BS, PG Diploma, Deep Learning (DL), Artificial Intelligence (AI), Software Engineering, Software Testing]
[ROW: PG Diploma (PGD), MTech level, MLOps, Mathematical Foundations of GenAI, Algorithms for Data Science]


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 20.2 Examination and Fee Structure]
20.2 Examination and Fee Structure


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.2 Examination and Fee Structure]
- Exam Schedule : Exams will be conducted on the scheduled final exam date of a term.
- Passing Requirement : To be eligible for continuation, students must appear for the final exam in all the subjects relevant to their readmission, as listed above.
- Academic Continuation :


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.2 Examination and Fee Structure]
- Students may register for the next-level courses irrespective of their performance in the readmission exams. However, attendance is mandatory.
- If a student scores less than 50% in any subject, that subject will be added to their academic load and treated as a pending course; Such courses will not be counted to the credits earned. The 50% cut-off is the same across all categories of students.


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.2 Examination and Fee Structure]
- Exam Fee : ₹5,000 per exam (irrespective of the number of subjects, be it 2/3/4)


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.2 Examination and Fee Structure]
- A 50% fee waiver is applicable for SC/ST candidates and PwD candidates with over 40% disability.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
Upon readmission, the following policies apply:


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
- Student Roll Number : Remains unchanged from the student’s original enrollment.
- Progress Card and Grade Card


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
- Only courses undertaken after readmission will be listed in the progress and grade cards provided after readmission. The courses done that were counted towards the previous Original Certificate will not be listed in this.
- Courses cleared through mandatory re-admission exams will be shown as completed but not counted for credits in the program.


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
- The status called “Lateral Entry” will be mentioned in both the Progress and Grade card to make it clear that the student had exited at some level with the Original Certificate and had chosen to re-enter at the next level.
- Certificates


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
- Provisional Certificate : No changes in process
- Original Certificate : The previously issued Original Certificate need not be returned to the institute.


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
- Time Limit and Term Break


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 20.3 Post-Readmission Academic and Administrative Processes]
- There is no restriction on when a student may apply for re-admission.
- The duration of the term(s) between exit and re-entry will be excluded from the overall program time limit. After accounting for this exclusion, all other time limit constraints of the overall program apply.dire
- A formal Readmission Letter will be issued to the student once the re-entry into the program is approved.

[SECTION: Apply for the MS program as a CFTI student if you have CGPA>= 8.0 in IITM’s BS program. (Campus program)]
1. GATE score is not required for this
2. You will have to go through the admission process which may involve a test and/or interview to be selected.
3. The program will involve at least 1 year of course work and another 1-2 years of research work.
4. Monthly stipend is provided by the institute.
5. Outcomes:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Apply for the MS program as a CFTI student if you have CGPA>= 8.0 in IITM’s BS program. (Campus program)]
1. Complete MS and apply for a job.
2. Upgrade and convert the MS into a PhD at IITM.
3. Complete the MS and apply for a PhD at IITM or outside.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Apply for the MS program as a CFTI student if you have CGPA>= 8.0 in IITM’s BS program. (Campus program)]
The Senate noted the omission of the CFTI clause and approved to include the CFTI clause in the minimum requirements for admission to MS admissions for the departments of MST and DSAI of IIT Madras


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Apply for the MS program as a CFTI student if you have CGPA>= 8.0 in IITM’s BS program. (Campus program)]
Check the link for more details : https://research.iitm.ac.in/


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Apply for the MS program as a CFTI student if you have CGPA>= 8.0 in IITM’s BS program. (Campus program)]
1. Apply to the MS program as a Project staff within IITM (Campus program)


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Apply to the MS program as a Project staff within IITM (Campus program)]
Apply to the MS program as a Project staff within IITM (Campus program)


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Apply to the MS program as a Project staff within IITM (Campus program)]
1. If you are appointed as a project staff in any project with faculty at IITM, you may be eligible for project admission to the MS program.
2. While you are project staff, you need to register for courses on campus, as approved by your project faculty.
3. You need to complete two approved courses at IITM with CGPA ≥ 7.5, and have obtained at least a ‘C’ grade in each course to be eligible to apply for admission.
4. You need not have a GATE score or a minimum CGPA in the IITM BS program for this mode of admission.
5. You have to go through the admission process as stipulated by the department, which can include a test and/or interview.
6. The institute does not pay you the stipend in this case, but you will be supported through the project. The payment you get from the project is fixed by the PI.
7. Outcomes:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Apply to the MS program as a Project staff within IITM (Campus program)]
1. Complete MS and apply for a job.
2. Complete the MS and apply for a PhD at IITM or outside.
3. Upgrade and convert the MS into a PhD at IITM.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Apply to the MS program as a Project staff within IITM (Campus program)]
1. Upgrade to the on-campus MS program


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Upgrade to the on-campus MS program]
Upgrade to the on-campus MS program


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Upgrade to the on-campus MS program]
1. When you have completed 106 credits and CGPA >=8, you can apply in May/Nov of every year through the form floated by the POD team for the direct upgrade to MS.
2. If selected, you will do the BS level courses on campus in person (16 credits of course work and 12 credits of project).
3. Selection to do the BS level will be through a test and/or interview.
4. Once the BS level of 28 credits is completed on campus and hence, the requirements of the BS degree, you will be assessed, and if found suitable, you will be admitted to the MS program. Stipend will be paid by the institute as per the prevalent norms. Fees to be paid for the MS program are given on the website.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Upgrade to the on-campus MS program]
Reference Doc : https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/e/2PACX-1vQYJJLnpZt6d0IvEVSx8bhzUFQ8hImK7sAVvSLxZ_6flZbtBp87hWKg2AuorYPFzquT1vQG-FerAUMy/pubhtml

e.  Upgrade to a PG Diploma or MTech degree online after the BS

[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
The program provides the students in the BS level of the program to pursue an apprenticeship with companies and research projects and earn up to 12 credits in this level. Doing an apprenticeship is optional.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
Steps involved in the Apprenticeship program:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Approval for the company/research project where the student can work as an apprentice


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
If the student applies and is accepted by any of the companies that are preapproved by IITM for this purpose, the apprenticeship will be directly approved.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
Else the student will have to submit the details of the company/organization and the Apprenticeship  committee will decide based on the details submitted.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Reporting Manager


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
Once the apprenticeship is approved, the student has to submit the details of the Reporting Manager in the Organization to IITM.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Logging the weekly work report


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
Students have to file the work log to the IITM BS office every week.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Review after first 4 weeks


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
After 4 weeks, based on the student’s performance,  the reporting manager has to confirm if they would like to continue with the student as an apprentice for the next 7 months. This confirmation letter should be submitted to the IITM BS Program’s Office.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Possible exit after 4 months


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
After the completion of 4 months, the student or the company can choose to discontinue the apprenticeship. A letter from the company has to be submitted outlining the work done by the student and whether it was satisfactory to obtain partial credits.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. After the completion of 8 months of internship


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
A letter from the Reporting Manager should be submitted to the IITM BS program on the company’s letterhead stating the work done by the student and whether it was satisfactory.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
The following will be norms with respect to pursuing an apprenticeship:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Number of courses that can be taken along while pursuing the apprenticeship


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
Students can pursue a maximum of 2 courses each term during the duration of the apprenticeship.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Duration of the apprenticeship that can be credited - 8 months, full time
2. Academic Credits


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. <4 months: 0 credits.
2. 4<duration<8 months: 4 credits
3. 8 months: 12 credits


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
1. Stipend : This is mutually agreed upon between the student and the company when finalizing the apprenticeship.
2. Mode of apprenticeship: Remote or On-site : This will depend on the company’s policy.
3. Pre-placement Offer : On successful completion of the apprenticeship, companies will have the option to give a pre-placement offer to the students.
4. Payment of fees:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
The fee for credit transfer is fixed as Rs 5000 /- per credit for Apprenticeship. When  students opt to credit this at the BS level, they have to pay the credit transfer fee at the time of registration.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 22. Apprenticeship in the BS level of IIT Madras’ BS in Data Science and Applications]
Please refer the link to know more about Apprenticeship: https://docs.google.com/document/d/e/2PACX-1vQ_ZCTw1_PzVDBJ7u-wX18KUgl-IK1POIYRvyjAYsj_A-tS99K7ARRuNVKk0tp34B1woqclNxvY_0Lc/pub


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23 . Software and Hardware Requirements - Mandatory System Specifications]
23 . Software and Hardware Requirements - Mandatory System Specifications


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 23 . Software and Hardware Requirements - Mandatory System Specifications]
The following are the essential requirements that are recommended to pursue the BSc Degree program from IIT Madras [1] . Please note that there will be updates in some of the specifications with changes happening in technology.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23.1 Hardware]
23.1 Hardware


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 23.1 Hardware]
To enroll and study in the program, every student is required to have a laptop or desktop with the minimum configuration being as follows:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 23.1 Hardware]
1. RAM Size - 8 GB or higher ( The ability to install more memory is desirable ).
2. Processor - Intel 8th Generation or AMD 4th Generation or upwards or its equivalent (The latest processor configuration is always recommended)
3. Storage - Minimum of 500 GB, Desirable of 1 TB. Having an SSD storage is desirable.
4. Screen size and resolution - Minimum of 13” for laptop and Minimum of 15” for desktop with 1080p
5. Webcamera, a mic and speaker or an earphone/headphone with mic.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23.2 Software/Applications]
23.2 Software/Applications


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 23.2 Software/Applications]
1. Operating System - Minimum requirement of Windows 10 or Ubuntu LTS Version 20.04 (or any equivalent) or Mac OS Mojave. Having the capability of dual boot is desirable. If there is no capability for dual boot, then the operating system must support virtualization software like VirtualBox using which Ubuntu 20.04 can be emulated. The System Commands course in Semester 4 will be taught primarily on Ubuntu 20.04.
2. Browser - Latest version of Chrome with Google Account signed in
3. Basic familiarity with Google Suite of tools (Docs, Sheets and Slides), specifically collaboration features.
4. Any other software that is specified within the course


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23.3 Internet Bandwidth]
23.3 Internet Bandwidth


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 23.3 Internet Bandwidth]
A minimum of 2 MBPS connection is required to attend sessions without disruptions. However we strongly recommend broadband connections with much higher bandwidth for the best learning experience.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23.4 For Online Interactions/Proctored Examinations]
23.4 For Online Interactions/Proctored Examinations


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 23.4 For Online Interactions/Proctored Examinations]
1. Browser - Latest version of Chrome is desirable
2. Mobile with a front camera and good internet connection (VOLTE connections are ideal)
3. Mic and speaker to be able to speak to and listen to the person at the other end
4. Any applications as required to be installed for the interactions/examinations


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23.5 System compatibility test]
23.5 System compatibility test


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 23.5 System compatibility test]
All students have to mandatorily attend the system compatibility test and ensure that the system you have conforms to the above requirements and student has to participate in this on the dates mentioned by the Admin team to get this completed.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 23.6 Other References]
23.6 Other References


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 23.6 Other References]
Some more helpful links that will help you in understanding possible system configurations:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 23.6 Other References]
1. https://towardsdatascience.com/20-necessary-requirements-of-a-perfect-laptop-for-data-science-and-machine-learning-tasks-7d0c59c3cb63
2. https://www.practicaldatascience.org/html/buying_datascience_computer.html

[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 25. Research Paper Publishing Policy]
25. Research Paper Publishing Policy


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 25. Research Paper Publishing Policy]
Below are the points to be followed while publishing a Research paper :


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 25. Research Paper Publishing Policy]
Use of Institute Name : Students are not permitted to use “IIT Madras” or “IIT Madras BS Degree” as their affiliation in any research publication unless the work has been carried out under the guidance of a faculty member/instructor officially associated with the Institute.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 25. Research Paper Publishing Policy]
Independent Work : If students wish to publish independent work (without faculty involvement), they must publish under their personal capacity, without citing "IIT Madras" or "IIT Madras BS Degree" as their affiliation.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 25. Research Paper Publishing Policy]
Any unauthorised use of the institute’s name in publications will be treated as a violation and may invite disciplinary action as severe as cancellation of registration.

[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: 29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]]
29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]]
The BS committee has asked to implement additional verification of the income documents before providing the institute fee waiver to students whose total family income is less than ₹5 LPA. This fee is applicable to students in the diploma and degree levels who wish to avail the fee waivers based on economic criteria.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]]
As part of this process, learners will be required to submit the relevant Income Tax Return (ITR) and/or Form 26AS of themselves, their parents, and/or spouse , as applicable. These documents will be collected separately from the students. This additional verification is based on the observation that a significant number of students have not submitted adequate or appropriate income documents while claiming fee waivers based on the total family income.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]]
Upon completion of the verification


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]]
- If the submitted documents are approved , the applicable fee waiver will be granted.
- If the submitted documents are rejected , the fee waiver will be withdrawn, and the student must pay the course fees as per their economic category.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 29. Mandatory submission by students (Updated on July 24, 2026) 29.1 Fee Waiver Verification Process WEF Jan 2026 term [updated on Feb 18,2026]]
Note: This additional income verification is applicable for Diploma and Degree level students only

[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Step 1: Raise a Support Ticket]
Step 1: Raise a Support Ticket


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Step 1: Raise a Support Ticket]
All queries, requests, and issues must first be submitted through the Student Support Portal by raising a support ticket.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Step 1: Raise a Support Ticket]
Please wait for at least 24 hours after raising the ticket for the support team to review and respond.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Step 2: Follow Up on the Existing Ticket]
Step 2: Follow Up on the Existing Ticket


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Step 2: Follow Up on the Existing Ticket]
If you have not received a response within 24 hours, you may follow up on the same ticket instead of creating a new one.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Step 3: Escalation]
Step 3: Escalation


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Step 3: Escalation]
If you have already raised a support ticket, waited for 24 hours, and followed up on the ticket, but have still not received a response or are not satisfied with the resolution provided, you may escalate the matter as per the escalation matrix below.


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Level 1 Escalation]
Level 1 Escalation


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Level 1 Escalation]
Email: akshaya@study.iitm.ac.in , nkavitha@code.iitm.ac.in


[TYPE: heading]

Level 2 Escalation


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Level 2 Escalation]
Email: bharathi@study.iitm.ac.in


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Level 2 Escalation]
Students are requested to contact the above email IDs first. If the issue remains unresolved or the response received is not satisfactory, it may be escalated further as per the hierarchy below.


[TYPE: heading]

Level 3 Escalation

Level 4 Escalation : Dean (Academics)


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Level 5 Escalation : Director, IIT Madras]
Level 5 Escalation : Director, IIT Madras


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Important Guidelines]
Important Guidelines


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: Important Guidelines]
- Always mention your Support Ticket Number while escalating an issue.
- Escalations without a previously raised support ticket may not be entertained.
- Please allow the concerned team adequate time to review and respond before proceeding to the next level of escalation.
- Multiple emails or repeated escalations to different teams regarding the same issue may result in delays in resolution.
- The escalation matrix must be followed sequentially.
- Escalations should be used only when the issue remains unresolved or the response received is not satisfactory.

[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
34 . Common FAQ’s


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
Updated on JAN 05,2026


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
1. After exiting at the Diploma level, will I receive alumni status , along with an alumni card ?


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
No, alumni status is only if you complete BSc or higher.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
1. The exit form deadline mentioned is the 12th January , but the email specifies only BSc/BS . Does this same deadline also apply to Diploma level exit ?


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
Yes, same deadline.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
1. If I plan to exit, do I still need to pay the RKA fee for the upcoming term before filling the exit form?


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
Not required.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
1. Will I be eligible for any placement support , until I receive my certificate, considering that I already hold another degree?


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
Please write to iic@study.iitm.ac.in for this.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
FAQ on project courses :


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
1. Can project improvement only be done after completing all the projects once or can I do it before that if I know that I won't be able to clear the cutoff for the BSc level ? The project improvement can be done only after completion all 4 projects and the project cgpa < 7


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
2. Can project improvement only be done for 1 project ? Project improvement can be taken more than once for the respective project course provided if you have terms left for diploma completion and terms left to enter degree level.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
3. If not, can I only take a single project in a term for improvement or multiple projects ? You can take more than 1 project for improvement


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
4. If I chose a project for improvement and then for some reason am unable to complete it or get a U or low grade then will I be able to use the previous scores or not ?


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]
If you get a U grade or I grade in project improvement then the previous pass grade is only considered.

[SECTION: 34 . Common FAQ’s]

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: General]
Students who were OPPE eligible and received an I_OP in the May 2026 term, and have registered as Repeat OPPE for the Sep 2026 term, will continue to be considered eligible for OPPE in Sep 2026 . However, students who were marked OPPE Ineligible with I_OP or I_Both and have registered for the respective course as Repeat OPPE or Repeat OPPE & End Term must regain OPPE eligibility in the Sep 2026 term by submitting the required assignments and OPPE SCT. If OPPE eligibility is not attained, the OPPE will not be scheduled and a U grade will be awarded for the Sep 2026 term.

[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Bonus Marks


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Bonus Marks are for encouraging and incentivising the students to participate more in the activities of the course.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
These marks will be added ONLY for the students who pass the course and will only impact the course grade, and not the pass criteria . Criteria for bonus marks:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Marks will be applicable based on availability of mock activities in the course. It can vary from course to course and can range from 0-2.


[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
- If the average of the marks obtained in all the mock tests conducted before Quiz 1 & Quiz 2 >= 40/100, students will get 2 marks. The mock tests have to be done within the time specified.
- The course team may set up additional activities in the course which will be eligible for up to 3 bonus marks. If no additional activities are set up, this bonus won't be applicable.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Participation in the discourse forum: Badges


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
We will be looking at active engagement with Discourse in a term and provide badges for participation.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Badges will be given based on the number of hours a student spends on Discourse. A student is expected to spend a minimum of 1 hour per week per course in Discourse reading posts.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Student will get the following badges:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Read time of up to 4 hours per course in a term: Badge 1


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Read time of up to 8 hours per course in a term: Badge 2


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
Read time of up to 12 hours per course or more: Badge 3


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Bonus Marks]
(Applicable only after you get the BS student roll number)


[TYPE: heading]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
Information about course grades:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
S,A,B,C,D,E - Pass grade; U/WA/WQ - Fail grade ;  I - Incomplete


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
In I grade - there are 3 types:


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
1. If you are absent for ET alone but all other assessment components are completed (quizzes, OPPEs, project, weekly assignments) - grade will be pushed as I.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
Options: You can register to take up the ET exam alone in the subsequent term. OPPE and quizzes will not be scheduled for you. All other marks for Final course score will be taken from the previous run. The fee will be charged only for the end term exam.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
OR


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
You can choose to repeat the entire course paying the full course fees.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
1. If you have failed in the OPPE but wrote the ET exam and crossed the cutoff for T as given in the course grading policy, grade = I_OP.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
You can register to do the OPPE alone whereby if the course has one OPPE, that will be scheduled. If the course has 2 OPPEs, both will be scheduled for you. You can attempt the OPPE alone. All other mark components of Final course score will be taken from the previous run. ET and quizzes will not be scheduled for you.


[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
1. If you have failed in the OPPE and absent for ET, then grade = I_BOTH


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
You can register for ET and OPPEs alone. If the course has one OPPE, that will be scheduled. If the course has 2 OPPEs, both will be scheduled for you. You will attempt the ET and OPPEs. Quiz and GAA and other components will be taken from the previous run and not scheduled.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Information about course grades:]
In all the above cases, you can also choose to Repeat the entire course and not just do the ET/OPPE alone if you want to reattempt all assessment components.

[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:]
Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:]
For those entering Foundation Level in MAY 2024 or after, kindly go through the new rules: 4/6/8 courses to be completed in 4/6/9 terms. Else you will be removed from the program.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:]
Academic policies:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:]
1. In each programming assignment, be it any course or any OPPE,  taking help from LLMs (e.g. ChatGPT, Gemini) partially or completely is considered plagiarism.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:]
2. Unless explicitly permitted, do not use LLMs. Using LLMs is considered a violation of honour code.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Foundation level courses:]
3. Students can discuss and learn from each other but the assignments are expected to be done individually based on their understanding.

Academic policies


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 7 . Programming in Python (CS1002)]
1. In each programming assignment, be it any course or any OPPE,  taking help from LLMs (e.g. ChatGPT, Gemini) partially or completely is considered plagiarism.

[SECTION: 7 . Programming in Python (CS1002)]
2. Unless explicitly permitted, do not use LLMs. Using LLMs is considered a violation of honour code.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 7 . Programming in Python (CS1002)]
3. Students can discuss and learn from each other but the assignments are expected to be done individually based on their understanding.

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 7 . Programming in Python (CS1002)]
Repeat_OPPE Criteria (w.e.f from Jan 2026 term) [updated on Jan 05, 2026]


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 7 . Programming in Python (CS1002)]
Students who were OPPE eligible and received an I_OP in the May 2026 term, and have registered as Repeat OPPE for the Sep 2026 term, will continue to be considered eligible for OPPE in Sep 2026. However, students who were marked OPPE Ineligible with I_OP or I_Both and have registered for the respective course as Repeat OPPE or Repeat OPPE & End Term must regain OPPE eligibility in the Sep 2026 term by submitting the required assignments and OPPE SCT. If OPPE eligibility is not attained, the OPPE will not be scheduled and a U grade will be awarded for the Sep 2026 term.

[ROW: 3., <35, U, Repeat the entire course]

[ROW: 6, Absent, -, I_BOTH, Complete ET and OPE in next term, Both oppes will be scheduled. GA and quiz marks will be carried over OR Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 7, Present Score > =40/100, Absent, -, I, Complete ET alone in next term; OPPE will NOT be scheduled. GA, quiz and OPPE marks will be carried over OR Repeat the entire course]

[TYPE: ordered_list]

[TYPE: ordered_list]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Diploma level courses:]
1. If you have 2 courses with OPPEs, both will be scheduled on Sunday, December 20, 2026 .
2. If you have 3 courses with OPPEs, OPPEs for 2 courses will be scheduled on Sunday, December 20, 2026 and OPPE for 1 course will be scheduled on Sunday, January 3, 2027 .
3. If you have 4 courses with OPPEs, OPPEs for 2 courses will be scheduled on Sunday, December 20, 2026 and OPPE for the other 2 courses will be scheduled on Sunday, January 3, 2027


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Diploma level courses:]
In the cases of b and c above, if you fail in subjects of Sunday, December 20, 2026 and are rescheduled for Sunday, January 3, 2027 , there will be no option to split the subjects any further or reschedule them. All will have to be written on the same day.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Suggested pathway to register and study Diploma level courses:]
Only if you have a conflicting engagement on Sunday, December 20, 2026 , which cannot be changed or moved and you submit a request to us with proof, we will check the proofs and if found valid, we will make changes to the above allocation. Since both Sunday, December 20, 2026 and Sunday, January 3, 2027 are Sundays, please ensure you keep it free, especially as per the time slots given here for giving the exams.

[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 8. Database management s ystem (DBMS) - Diploma in Programming  (CS2001)]
- Students have to mandatorily attend the OPPE on the first date. If you fail in this, you get a chance to reappear the next weekend. So you get 2 chances to attempt the exam.
- If you are absent on the first date, you do not get the reattempt chance.
- If the first date is inconvenient due to a clash with some other engagement, then you can apply for a postponement to the second date providing sufficient proof. If this is accepted, your exam will be moved to the second date but if you fail in this or are absent, you will not get another chance to reattempt. You will have to do it in the subsequent term .

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
Students who were OPPE eligible and received an I_OP in the May 2026 term, and have registered as Repeat OPPE for the Sep 2026 term, will continue to be considered eligible for OPPE in Sep 2026. However, students who were marked OPPE Ineligible with I_OP or I_Both and have registered for the respective course as Repeat OPPE or Repeat OPPE & End Term must regain OPPE eligibility in the Sep 2026 term by submitting the required assignments and OPPE SCT. If OPPE eligibility is not attained, the OPPE will not be scheduled and a U grade will be awarded for the Sep 2026 term.

[TYPE: unordered_list]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
- If you fail in this, you get a chance to reappear the next weekend. So you get 2 chances to attempt the exam.
- If the first date is inconvenient due to a clash with some other engagement, then you can apply for a postponement to the second date providing sufficient proof. If this is accepted, your exam will be moved to the second date but if you fail/absent in this, you will not get another chance to reattempt. You will have to do it in the subsequent term.
- If you are absent on 20th Dec, you DON'T get another chance on Jan 3rd..

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
BPTA: Average of the 4 BPT scores


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
QZ1 = score in Quiz I (0, if not attempted)


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
OPPE = Score in online remote proctored programming exam


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
F = score in End Term exam


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 11. System commands (Diploma in programming)  (SE2001)]
Final course score T = 0.05 GAA + 0.25 Qz1 + 0.3 OPPE + 0.3F + 0.1 BPTA

[HEADERS: , OPE, ET, T, Grade, ]
[ROW: 1., Absent/In Eligible, Absent/In Eligible, -, U, Repeat entire course]
[ROW: 2., Absent (OPE=0), Present, >=35, I_OP, Complete the BPT and OPE in next term; BPTs will be eligibility to write OPPE GA, quiz and ET marks will be carried over OR Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 3., <35, U, Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 4, Present score< 40/100, Present, >=40, I_OP, Complete the BPT and OPE in next term; BPTs will be eligibility to write OPPE GA, quiz and ET marks will be carried over OR Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 5, <40, U, Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 6, Absent, -, I_Both, Redo End term exam and OPE in next term; For doing the OPE, BPTs have to be done and students have to become eligible GA, quiz will be carried over OR Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 7, Present Score > = 40/100, Absent, -, I, Complete ET alone in next term; OPPE will NOT be given in this case. GA, quiz and OPPE marks will be carried over OR Repeat the entire course]
[ROW: 8, Present, , , Grade as per the Total score T]

Students are requested to complete the projects at least two terms before their diploma completion term i.e DO NOT REGISTER OR HAVE PROJECTS ALONE LEFT IN YOUR LAST TERM OF DIPLOMA.

Degree Level courses


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Level of the course:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
The first digit of the 4 digits given in the course code represents the level of the course.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Eg: CT is CS1001 is a 1 level course.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Deep Learning CS3004 is a 3 level course.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Industry 4.0 MS4001 is a 4 level course.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
BSc level:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
It is now mandatory that students have to complete both the core pairs and SPG in the BSc level. Credits = 20


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
All remaining 8 credits can be earned from IITM BS courses or NPTEL (maximum upto 4 credits from Table 2 ) or if there is an option to credit campus courses (IITM or other institutes), that too can be done.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
BS level:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
It is implemented wef Sep 2023 for the batch that enters the degree level in Sep 2023.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
2 courses in the level 4 or higher should be mandatorily completed in each of the BP and BD categories. Please refer course category in table1


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Out of the remaining 12 c redits, it is MANDATORY to earn 4 credits in the HS/MG category. HS/MG can come from inhouse electives or from NPTEL-Table 3 .


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Remaining credits are from any of the other in-house/campus electives or Apprenticeship electives.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Apprenticeship:


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]

[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
Academic policies


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
1. In each programming assignment, be it any course or any OPPE,  taking help from LLMs (e.g. ChatGPT, Gemini) partially or completely is considered plagiarism.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
2. Unless explicitly permitted, do not use LLMs. Using LLMs is considered a violation of honour code.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
3. Students can discuss and learn from each other but the assignments are expected to be done individually based on their understanding.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: Degree Level courses]
4. Please do not share your assignments with others before the deadlines. If similarities are found between submissions, all will be penalised irrespective of who did it first and who shared it with whom.

If OPPE eligibility is not attained, the OPPE will not be scheduled and a U grade will be awarded for the Sep 2026 term.

Academic policies


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18. Design Thinking for Data-Driven App Development (MS4002)]
1. In each programming assignment, be it any course or any OPPE,  taking help from LLMs (e.g. ChatGPT, Gemini) partially or completely is considered plagiarism.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18. Design Thinking for Data-Driven App Development (MS4002)]
2. Unless explicitly permitted, do not use LLMs. Using LLMs is considered a violation of honour code.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18. Design Thinking for Data-Driven App Development (MS4002)]
3. Students can discuss and learn from each other but the assignments are expected to be done individually based on their understanding.


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 18. Design Thinking for Data-Driven App Development (MS4002)]
4. Please do not share your assignments with others before the deadlines. If similarities are found between submissions, all will be penalised irrespective of who did it first and who shared it with whom.

- GAA = Average score in First 10 weekly graded assignments
- Qz1 = score in Quiz I (0, if not attempted)
- Qz2 = score in Quiz II (0, if not attempted)
- F = score in final exam


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 36 . Computer Organization (EE2106)]
T=0.05 GAA + max(0.6F + 0.25max(Qz1,Qz2),(0.5F+0.2Qz1+0.25Qz2))


[TYPE: paragraph]
[SECTION: 36 . Computer Organization (EE2106)]
Across Quiz-1, Quiz-2, and the End-Term exam, 5% of the questions will be direct questions from the Graded Assignments. (Though the W11/W12 assignment score is not included in GAA, W11/W12 contents will be included for the final exam. Hence, please practice and submit W11/W12 assignment).
